## Cloak of gaer

Cloak of gaer was a powerful spell used by clerics of Lolth.

Effects
Cloak of gaer provided the caster or a targeted creature with significant healing powers. When cast, the targeted creature was enveloped by a faint aura. The spell was very powerful, capable of even regenerating severed or missing extremities. Not only did cloak of gaer heal, it also provided protection against every type of poison, parasite and disease as well as the effects of charm or insanity-based spells. It also protected against external mental influences, curses and geas.

Once cast, the spell rejuvenated the cast or targeted creature. They became more alert and felt no pain. If exhausted they felt fully awake and if they were inebriated, full sobriety was restored.

The spell healed any damage the caster or targeted creature received. If cast after major trauma, the spell fully healed any injuries. However, the individual had to survive the intense shock to the system.

The spell was so powerful it could even restore life to a very recently decease individual. Dispel magic had no effect on the spell.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components: four drops of the caster's blood, four drops of dew and four drops of holy water.

## Clan Icehammer

Clan Icehammer was an extended family of dwarves that hailed from Icespire Peak in the Sword Mountains during the 14 and 15 centuries DR.

Activities
The dwarves of Clan Icehammer were avid and successful miners.

Base of Operations
They dwelled within the small village of Torunedar on the mountain's surface, but had grand plans to construct a mighty underground fortress within.

Possessions
The eponymous weapon known as the Icehammer was a dwarven throwing hammer long-associated with the clan with which it shared its name.

Relationships
Over time, they developed immense enmity towards frost giants and goblins that also dwelled on and within Icespire Peak.

History
At some point during the late 14 century DR, the frost giant Lakkar acquired the Winterforge, a powerful artifact associated with the Elemental Chaos. Lakkar waged war on the local clan of Icehammer dwarves armed with the powerful weapon bestowed upon him by Thrym himself. The conflict came to an end when Skeld Icehammer, the formidable giant slayer, threw the Icehammer into Lakkar's heart, killing the giant and scattering his army to the surrounding lands.

Years later, Skeld's son Florin founded the village of Torunedar on Icespire Peak with the hope and promise of a better life for them all. The dwarves sought to create a new home for their clan and reclaim his father's Icehammer, which remained stuck within the Winterforge inside the mountain. They discovered rich mithral deposits within Icespire Peak, dug out mines to retrieve the valuable metal, and thrived for years in their new home.

Unfortunately, Florin's expedition to the Winterforge was not as successful. When he uncovered the Winterforge altar, Florin attempted to retrieve the Icehammer, but in doing so disrupted the long-dormant magic within. Soon after, the frost giant Hrimnir eventually subjugated the goblins of the local Winterkill tribe and ordered them to raid the Icehammer holdings. The giant and goblinoid forces took over the dwarves' mines as their own and overran Torunedar. Florin refused to let his people abandon Icespire Peak entirely, and struck out against Hrimnir and his minions in an ongoing guerilla conflict.

At some point during or around the, the Icehammer dwarves found a new ally an an adventurer from Neverwinter. Working together, the adventurer and Florin along with his clanmates managed to drive the Winterkill goblins from Torunedar and reclaim both their mithral mines and the Icehammer itself. They were then able to strike out against the frost giants, slaying Hrimnir's lieutenants and then the giant leader himself.

Later one in the 1480S DR, the Cult of the Dragon disrupted Clan Icehammer's mithral mining as part of their ongoing effort to accumulate wealth and perform a ritual to summon Tiamat to the Realms. The same hero that fought alongside the Icehammer dwarves returned to aide them once again, working alongside Fitzhugh Montgomery of the Lords' Alliance. Together they defeated Dragonsoul Theures from the dwarven mines and exiling the cult from Icespire Peak once and for all.

## Clenched Fist

The Clenched Fist was a fist-shaped enchanted mace created sometime before 1312 DR.

Description
The heavy mace was a rusty iron scepter created in the form of a hand curled into a fist.

Powers
The Clenched Fist possessed a simple +1 enchantment. However, the main curios magic of the Fist was its ability to generate a single gold coin per day. When the wielder of the weapon held it in their left hand and put their right palm underneath the Fist, the iron hand opened up and propped a gold piece into the wielder's palm. This ability was referred to as the gold blessing.

History
Clenched Fist's origins were a mystery. Some believed that the mace was a scepter and the symbol of office in some old kingdom; however, which court or church it might've once belonged to was unknown. It was very likely, that the Clenched Fist was many hundreds of years old by the 14 century DR. By the, Clenched Fist found its way into a small logging village of Shaengarne Ford in Icewind Dale, where it could be found in the town's watermill.

## Cloak of Mysteries

The Cloak of Mysteries was an artifact of the Leiran faith from the Hidden House of Leira in Westgate.

Powers
The Cloak of Mysteries was not just a festive religious garb, it also possessed a powerful +3 enchantment that deflected harm from the wearer. Additionally, the item's magics gave the wearer innate knowledge and focus in illusion school of magics and dramatically increased their ability to bluff.

History
The Hidden House of Leira in Westgate's Undergate, was a busy center of worship of the Lady of Deception. The Cloak of Mysteries was a celebrated ceremonial garb cherished by the Church of Leira and worn by high-ranking clerics during important festivals and ceremonies of the faith. When the goddess was believed to have been slain by god Cyric, her temple in Westgate was abandoned and the Cloak of Mysteries – lost, thought destroyed.

In reality, the Cloak of Mysteries was hidden by the clerics of Leira within the trapped and illusion-filled abandoned Hidden House of Leira.

In the, the Cloak of Mysteries was found and claimed by the oni mage Skyrim, who took the ruined temple as his lair. Subsequently, the Cloak of Mysteries was taken from defeated oni by Garrett the Bold and his hired hobyguard adventurers. It was unknown if the adventurers slain Garrett and kept the cloak for themselves, or of the rogue was allowed to claim his bounty from the ruined temple.

Description
This beautiful cloak was made out of strange glowing fabric.

The Cloak of Mists granted the wearer improved defenses against mundane or magical threats and effects. The wearer also was granted the ability to summon a powerful gust of wind once per day.

A strange enigmatic being only known as Metastakixx was trapped in an ancient temple in one of the caves in the Crab's Claw Coast. The being's history was unknown; however, it was believed that Metastakixx was an incredibly old creature tasked with protecting remnants of an old and forgotten deity. As of the, Metastakixx was still bound to the temple and waited for the one person destined to let them free. If the strange being was to be freed from the eternal bondage, the Cloak of Mists was sure to be a reward for the kind deed, despite freedom being Metastakixx's doom.

## Cloak of One Plume

The Cloak of One Plume was a legendary artifact of pluma magic from the continent of Maztica.

Description
The Cloak of One Plume appeared to have been crafted out of a single giant feather of a multitude of colors.

Powers
When worn, this powerful enchanted cape affected all surrounding creatures, making the wearer appear highly charismatic and an inspiring leader. The effects of the cape were somewhat similar to the effects of the enthrall spell. All those around the bearer of the Cloak of One Plume bravely followed them into battle, inspired by its magic. However, the Cloak of One Plume could only bestow this inspiring magic onto inhabitants of the True World who were born on the continent of Maztica. The wearer of the cape was also given the ability to fly once per day. Additionally, the wearer was granted magical resistance to fire, both natural and supernatural. The Cloak of One Plume was also big enough to cover one additional humanoid when faced with deadly fires.

History
When The Silent Counselor of the Gods, Qotal, departed Maztica, he left a prophecy of his eventual return. The Cloak of One Plume would be worn by the Chosen Daughter of Qotal, guiding his return to the True World. Many centuries later, the Chosen Daughter began walking the path that eventually led to the deity's return. The young woman's name was Erixitl. In the, soon after she met with the first prophesied harbinger—the couatl named Chitikas—Erixitl received a gift from a blind featherworker in Nexal. She wore the cloak without realizing its true wondrous nature. The Chosen Daughter wore the Cloak of One Plume into the calamity known as the Night of Wailing.

During the explosion of Mount Zatal on the Night of Wailing, the Cloak of One Plume was believed to have been incinerated. It was not true. The artifact was rescued by a group of priests of Azul who stashed it away within a cave on the slopes of Mount Zatal. The deity Azul summoned a rain dragon servant to guard the Cloak of One Plume. The cleric who hid the cape died soon after, but the fellow clergy members continued to pay tributes to the guardian, ignorant of what was being guarded.

In the, rumors of the Cloak of One Plume surviving the fires of Zatal reached Governor-General Cordell of Helmsport. He ordered a band of adventurers to retrieve the artifact under the guise of benevolent action, that is, securing items from monsters who flooded the ruined city of Nexal and ne'er-do-wells who arrived from Faerûn. Cordell offered adventurers gold, freedom, land, and title in New Amn. However, Cordell was not the only power that desired the prophesied cloak. DaNosta, the captain of Fort Cordell attempted to befriend adventurers in order to steal or cheat the Cloak of One Plume from them, while Maztican-born Revered Counselor Caxal of Payit wanted to claim the artifact for his people and his deity—the creator of the cloak, Qotal.

The cloak was recovered with help from Chioptl the tabaxi. However, it remained unclear who of the three men if any, ended up claiming the Cloak of One Plume.

## Clockwork dragon

Clockwork dragons were a variety of clockwork constructs made to resemble dragons.

Description
They resembled finely crafted statues plated in the metals that corresponded to the varieties of metallic dragons, including brass, bronze, copper, silver, or faux gold.

Their size often varied, ranging from about the size of any humanoid to that of any actual adult dragon.

Abilities
These draconic creations were exceptionally intelligent and made for superb guardians. They could be "programed" to take a series of intricate actions depending on the circumstances surrounding any potential encounter.

Like other constructs, clockwork dragons had no need to breathe, sleep, or eat for sustenance. They were however vulnerable to any forms of magic or other powers that affected true, living breathing dragons.

Combat
Clockwork dragons fought in a similar manner to their organic inspirators. They could bite or claw opponents and use their enormous wings to knock others down with their mere presence.

They also possessed dragons' breath weapons, though theirs came in two varieties. Clockwork dragons could breathe lightning—in a manner similar to blue dragons―or "repulsion energy", which sent their enemies flying back upwards of .

While in their preferred lair, clockwork dragons could create an obfuscating cloud of fog or blasts of sound that resembled the clap of thunder.

History
As of the, several humanoid-sized clockwork dragons were stationed outside the Orrery Vault as guardians.

Notable Clockwork Dragons
 Timekeeper, an Abeiran clockwork dragon that dwelled in the ruined city of Xorvintroth.

## Clion

Clion was a young fighter and thief inhabiting the city of Waterdeep during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Clion joined a group of warrior-thieves who called themselves the Sword Ghosts.

History
He was originally from Luskan.

Relationships
Clion was life-long friends with Raymid. Other close associates of his included Arilyn Moonblade, Olophin, and Tomed Myntion. Ariyln was impressed with his raw skill with a blade and sent him to the Academy of Arms to hone his skill further under Kymil Nimesin. Clion joined the City Watch of Waterdeep to repay Kymil for the training.

## Claw of Nezram

The claw of Nezram was a unique magic item enchanted by the wizard Nezram.

Description
The claw was a replica of a humanoid hand that was made from ivory and studded with rubies. Sharp talons stood in place of regular fingernails.

Powers
The claw was an intelligent sentient item that initially battled the will of anyone trying to control it.

The claw of Nezram possessed several powers. The most mundane of these was scratching any itch the wielder had when commanded to do so. The claw could be order to attack enemies in combat, slashing at them and attempting to grip their throats where it would squeeze until the target died. If the claw latched onto a target's weapon hand then they were unable to use that hand in combat. The claw could also glow red if rubies were nearby.

History
Five claws of Nezram were known to exist. All were held in the possession of the powerful wizard Hodkamset.

## Clout

Clouts were undergaments worn by most folks of the Realms. They were made of a tight belt worn around the hips, and of a long strip of cloth passed between the legs, slipped in the belt on the front and back of the body, and left to dangle.

Description
The belt of the clouts could be manufactured from various different materials: the most durable were made of leather, but cheaper alternatives could be made of cloth or even cord. They were usually closed on the front using small hooks.

The strip of cloth passed between the legs was made of fabric, usually cotton. The dangling ends were often designed to be tied together, and was folded on itself by horse riders to provide additional padding and absorbancy.

While most clouts were basic undergarments, some were decorative, made "to be seen", or even sold freshly-perfumed. Such novelty items could be found in big cities such as Waterdeep.

## Cloedraen Baerlbohun

Cloedraen Baerlbohun was one of the children of "Toadroarer" Phonchandas Baerlbohun, the foul-tempered self-made noble of Baerlbohun Towers in Athkatla, active in the 14 century DR.

Personality
Cloedraen was a spoiled individual who only cared about wenching, drinking, and dueling. Like his father and brothers, he had a foul temper and was prone to smashing things in fits of rage.

History
By the late 14, Cloedraen's mother and two other wives of "Toadroarer" were dead from various causes. The Baerlbohun family was looked down upon by other merchant and noble families of Athkatla, while Cloedraen's uncle's branch of the family that resided within Baerlbohun House pretended that Phonchandas's branch did not exist. Cloedraen's branch returned the favor.

## Clean (orison)

Clean was an abjuration orison.

Effects
When cast, this spell cleaned the targeted surface, such as a floor, wall, window and even dirty dishes. The spell was capable of removing dirt, soil and grease. It could be used to remove pigments but special care was required.

## Clockwork soul

Clockwork soul sorcerers were those individual whose arcane powers originated from the universal force of order, that permeated some planes of existence such as Mechanus.

Description
The origin of these sorcerers' powers were evident by the 'manifestations of order' that appeared on or around their body. Some clockwork soul sorcerers were born with brass-colored skin or appeared to have ticking clock faces within their eyes. Yet others manifested spectral geometric figures, or even interlinking cogs accompanied with the phantom sound of whirring machinery.

Culture
Some of these sorcerers had ancestors who had involved themselves in the affairs of the modron, and had even participated in one of the Great Modron Marches.

Abilities
Individuals with clockwork souls strived to maintain order in the multiverse. By focusing their will for just a moment, these sorcerers could lessen the chance of any severely adverse or truly fantastic outcome that may have resulted from someone else's actions.

As stewards of cosmic law, clockwork souls could also manifest magical barriers that prevented an individual from harm. They did so by drawing from what was known as the "grand equation of existence." Those sorcerers that tapped deeper into their innate power could even link their consciousness into the never-ending mathematical calculations that originated from Mechanus itself.

The most powerful of these sorcerers could even conjure spirits that resembled modrons or other constructs within a  cube. These phantasmal conjurations traveled as directed, healing the wounds of the sorcerer's allies, repairing and items or devices they passed over, and even nullifying any magic of a certain potency or lesser.

## Cloaks (food)

Cloaks were one of the specialty side dishes served at the ramshackle Owlroost Head Inn of the Amnian town of Purskul as of the late 14 century DR.

Description
Cloaks were several side dishes served at the Owlroost Head Inn by themselves or as a main entrée. To create the first type of cloaks, one needed to cook, chop, and mash potatos, then shape them and fry until crispy on the outside and fluffy on the inside, all while flavoring them with lemon, flower buds, and rosemary. Another option were squid-stuffed yams. The squid was boiled in lime juice and the plate was then decorated with lemon spices and crushed mint. Lastly, a portion of fried bones salad was considered to be one of the inn's cloaks.

## Clockroach

Clockroaches, or Lantanese clockroaches, were constructs created as cleaning automatons for use in dungeons and workshops.

Description
Lantanese clockroaches were small bug-like constructs the size of a dog. Their faces sprouted sharp pincers and a nozzle that could discharge deadly acid and often dripped when inactive. Their outer shells were dotted with glass eyes allowing clockroaches to see in all directions simultaneously. An individual creature was  long and  wide. Their bodies were held  above ground by eight legs. Clockroaches constantly emitted the whirring noise of internal cogs and rotating parts.

Most commonly, clockroaches were made out of brass or bronze, with steel and iron specimens being a rare occurrence.

Abilities
Similarly to bugs, clockroaches could climb as fast as they could move and burrow through earth and stone with ease. This was thanks to clockroaches' legs being covered in tiny hooks.

Behavior
Clockroaches did not have autonomy. They were controlled by a command amulet and performed tasks assigned by an individual who possessed the amulet. In order to program a clockroach, its master had to place the command amulet into a slot on the construct's carapace. Instructions could be at most twenty-five words. One such amulet was valued at 2,150 gp.

Combat
Apart from their sharp pincers, clockroaches' main weapon was the magically-replenishing acid the creatures sprayed out of nozzles on their faces. The acid spray had the reach of . Clockroaches only attacked when explicitly instructed to do so, when ordered to clean an area, or in self-defense.

Ecology
Like other constructs, clockroaches needed no air, food, water, nor sleep.

History

Clockroaches originated from the island nation of Lantan – creations of Gond's Wondermakers. Due to their usefulness, clockroaches were exported out of Lantan and used across many places throughout the Sword Coast. One explorer attempted to use a horde of clockroaches to clear a way to a certain ruin in the jungles of Chult. The plan was an utter failure, unleashing a swarm of clockroaches into the wild with the detective to devour.

Circa the, clockroaches were used by the Lantanna artificers who ran an illegal logging operation in the jungles of Samarlogh. The Lantan-Samarach portal was at the center of the logging camp, guarded by numerous clockroaches, Lantanese blade spiders, and other constructs, maintained by Ahri "Golemkin". Some of the Lantanese clockroaches came in easily transportable collapsible configurations.

In the, a single clockroach belonged to Halaster's Heirs who resided on the Dungeon Level of the mega-dungeon underneath Waterdeep – Undermountain.

One of the heroes who returned to the tribe and brought the dire news – Nathani, secretly allied with the Mother of a Thousand Young and was the cause of Clymnas's capture. ## Cloak of arachnida

Cloaks of arachnida were enchanted cloaks that granted the wearer abilities of a spider.

Description
Cloaks of arachnida were black mantles that emanated a strong aura of alteration magic. They were often decorated with silken patterns of webs.

Powers
The wearers of these cloaks were granted the continual effects of the spider climb spell, allowing them to scale any surface, rendering them completely immune to the web spell, and unrestrainable by any mundane webs. Additionally, the wearer was able to create a magical web, double the size of such a web conjured via spell. Lastly, the wearer became resistant to any arachnid venom or poison.

Creation
Spiderstone golems were imbued with magics that lingered on their bodies after the constructs were destroyed. As such, remnants of spiderstone golems could be used in crafting and enchanting of spider-related magic items, including cloaks of arachnida. A skilled crafter of magic items could recreate a cloak of arachnida using spider climb and web spells.

History
In the, the so-called King of Spiders Sirachna gained entrance to a home on Westgate's Market Triangle from a cavern underneath. His arachnid pets ended up murdering a family who lived inside, leaving them haunting the home as restless ghosts. Some said the King of Spiders was killed by an adventurer, accompanied by Charissa Maernos, Mantides, and Rinara, who claimed his cloak of arachnida and several other magic items.

Notable Owners
Artemis Entreri, a late 14 century assassin.
Bazim-Gorag, a slaad lord, possessed a cloak of arachnida, stashed away in his treasure hoard.
Ezzat, a lich of Undermountain and once a Netherese mage.
Gof, a Grodd goblin general, active in the late 14 century DR.
Mez'Barris Armgo, the Matron Mother of House Barrison in the 14 center DR.
Yvonnel Baenre, the Mother Matron Baenre in the 14 center DR.

## Clavichord

A clavichord was a large keyboard instrument.

Description
These instruments were rather flat boxes of rectangular or oblong shape, measuring  wide and a little over  long. Their keyboard design was similar to that of their close relative the pipe organ, but in place of pipes they had horizontal metal strings like the harpsichord. But unlike the latter relative, these strings were struck by its internal mechanism rather than plucked.
Sound
Clavichords expressed soft and silvery tones, with the pitch varying depending upon how much force was struck upon the keys. They couldn't reach particularly loud volumes, being quieter than harpsichords.

Notable Users
Clavichords were often most appreciated by human communities and were a popular showpiece in merchant homes.
 The Bendekar's Mercantile storehouse in Ravens Bluff had a clavichord in their instruments collection.

## Cloning

[[File:Clone W&S.PNG|thumb|right|250px|Mordenkainen, examining a dumpy-looking clone created with a spell.]]
Cloning was the process of creating an exact duplicate of a living being, by replicating both their body and mind. There were several known ways of creating clones, also referred to as husks, each of which produced slightly different results.

Description
Clones differed in appearance, longevity, and consciousness, depending on the means in which they were created. One common characteristic among the various varieties of clones, was that they all appeared nearly identical to the person of which they were a copy.

Usage

The process of cloning was often used to preserve the consciousness and essence―in effect the very soul―of the recently deceased, specifically in cases where they could not be resurrected. Some well-funded adventurers were known to commission clones as a form "insurance" in the case of an unexpected and horrific death accompanied by dismemberment or loss of their corpse.

Wealthy and vain individuals sometimes used clones to retain their youthful appearance for years on end. They commissioned powerful wizards to create clones that aged at a slower rate that natural-born individuals.

While baelnorns did not make use of phylacteries as other liches, some were known to employ the use of clones.

Effects
The process of cloning could profoundly affect the psyche of both the target individual as well as the duplicate that was created. It was said that if both a living person and their clone existed at the same time, one or each individual would be afflicted with madness. More often than not the duplicate succumbed to this mental deterioration.

Some methods of cloning did not allow for the transferal of spellcasting abilities. Clones created by those means were readily more identifiable as duplicates, whenever it became apparent they could not wield magical powers as expected.

Certain powerful spells that compelled individuals to action—such as Geas/Quest―automatically transferred from a person to their clone.

Creation

Magic
There were several known ways to create a clone of a living person. The most well known means were two distinctly different necromancy spells, clone and stasis clone. The spell clone allowed a spellcaster to create a perfect copy of an individual, in body and mind, often to be used as a vessel for their consciousness in the case of their death. It could take up to four to eight months for a clone made in this way to fully mature.Stasis clone on worked in similar manner, with two key differences: the created clone remained preserved in a state of non-decay, so long as its progenitor remained alive, and the clone remained unaware of its origin, nor the existence of whom it duplicated.

There also existed a means to create clones using an ancient form of blood magic developed by the creator races. This process involved the blood of an individual, combined with necromantic magic, fed into a gruesome machine that spawned the clone. These clones did not exhibit the full range of personality as others, possessed no free will for much of their existence, and primarily sought only to serve their creator. The lifespans of these 'husk clones' lasted only months, thought it was believed that could be extended to years or even decades.

The shadovar archmage Telamont Tanthul was said to have developed a "multiple clone" spell that could grow numerous clones from a single appendage or even an object taken from an individual.

The Ice Queen Iyraclea had the ability to create a clone out of living ice, known as an iceborn.

Other
In the empire of Netheril, the arcanist Halavar developed a machine called a universal pantrograph that duplicated any item placed within, including organic material. This machine facilitated a more-perfect means of cloning, copying an individual and their personality with exacting accuracy. The device was unfortunately lost to the ages.

Centuries later, powerful individuals developed less more rudimentary means of non-magical cloning, using human-sized tanks filled with unidentified chemicals. The clones produced by these methods unfortunately shared the same limited consciousness and insanity as suffered by those created with magical spells. The alchemist Thessalar of the Temple of Moloch, and Jon Irenicus of Athkatla, both experimented with this method of cloning.

The Spire of Iriolarthas, which broke off from the Netherese enclave of Ythryn, house a rune-inscribed chamber that could transform illusory creatures into living beings. It was believed this room could transform simulacrums into living clones.

Ecology
Most societies across the Realms enacted laws that granted clones inheritance of their progenitor's estate. They were given full legal benefits as were granted to any other living citizen.

History

Long before the formation of Faerûn and other continents, during the Days of Thunder era, the ancient sarrukh experimented with dark, necromantic magic that allowed them to create 'blood copies' living beings. This form of cloning required only the blood of a living, sentient creature.

During the time of ancient Netheril, an ally of the archmage Chantonay created a clone spell-effect in the prison of Eileanar enclave. This echo effect created a duplicate of any person that came into contact with the spell ward''. Any clones created in this way had an expected life of several months, and had a significantly lower chance of succumbing to clone-induced insanity.

In the years leading up to the mid–to–late 14 century DR, Manshoon of the Zhentarim had focused much of his time and resources in perfecting the art of cloning. When Manshoon was slain his former allies Fzoul Chembryl and Lord Orgauth in the, about a dozen or so clones the Zhent leader awakened at once and immediately attacked his murderers. Malevolent archmages appeared across the Realms―in Cormyr, the Dalelands, Darkhold and beyond―each convinced they were the "real" Manshoon, and absolutely determined to kill any other fakes on sight. The subsequent conflicts that arose came to be known as the Manshoon Wars. After most of the clones were slain, it was believed that only three managed to survive the whole ordeal.

Decades later, sometime before the, the Tethyrian noblewoman Evonne Linden discovered secrets related to sarrukh cloning techniques in the jungles of Chult. She used the technology to transfer her spirit into the body of her daughter Ysabel. Unlike other clones that lasted only a few months, Evonne managed to live as her daughter for at least a decade's time.

During the late 15 century DR, the hags of the Sewn Sisters coven in the Tomb of the Nine Gods were known to create degraded clones of adventurers that ventured within the terrifying dungeon. Unlike other clones, these duplicates did not exactly resemble the adventurers and shared with them a mental link that could be exploited by their dark fey creators.

Notable Clones

 Alias, the living construct created by the disgraced Harper-turned-deity, Finder Wyvernspur
 Ellesime, several imperfect clones of the elven queen of Suldanessellar were made by her former partner Joneleth.
 Fai Chen, a famous planes-traveling merchant who cloned themself to escape the Wandering Emporium on Avernus.
 Halaster Blackcloak, the Mad Mage of Undermountain who created innumerable clones of himself.
 Jim Darkmagic, who was cloned after the death curse struck the Realms. The clone was subsequently traded by Omin Dran to an unnamed devil.
 Manshoon of Waterdeep, one of three surviving clones of Manshoon prime who went onto to lead the Zhentarim of Waterdeep in the late 15 century DR.
 Orbakh, the vampire regent of Westgate and clone of the original Manshoon, who was destroyed in a spell-duel with Elminster.
 Orlgaun, the black dragon that laired in the dungeons beneath Tower High in Zhentil Keep.
 Sememmon, Manshoon's appreciate who returned to life several times by the use of clones.
 Shradin Mulophor, the Netherese necromancer who served as the lord of Skullport in name only.
 Xeleth and Xedalli, the royal children of the elven Xaryxian Empire kept clones of themselves in case of their unfortunate deaths.

## Cleaver

A cleaver, also known as a meat cleaver, was a type of large culinary knife often used alongside a chopping block.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold cleavers at a cost of 8 silver pieces under its "Inn and Tavern" section, as well as casting molds for cleavers at a cost of 7 silver pieces under its "Laborers' List" section.

History
The existence of meat cleavers on Toril dated back as far as the Netherese Empire, where they were common on Eileanar.

The design of voulges were derived from peasants fastening meat cleavers to the ends of staves to form makeshift polearms.

Notable Users
Businesses
 Cleavers were a common tool of butchers and slaughterhouses, such as Alzael's Cleaver in Immersea.
 Black Rat, a tavern in Suzail.
 Whistling Wizard.
Groups
 The Vani, a tribe of gnomes living in the Samek Valley, utilized meat cleavers.
Individuals
 Alzael, the operator of Alzael's Cleaver.
 Armest Harrigo, a grocer in Waterdeep.
 Cookie, the ogre chef of The Overinn.
 Delgara Dauntsword was an expert with the cleaver, having frequently utilized it as a weapon back during her days of piracy.
 Gorstag, the innkeeper of The Rising Moon.
 Stamkk, a bugbear chef.
 Sull

Races & Sentient Creatures
 Ysgardian dwarves often forged and sold cleavers to Ysgardian trolls.
Regions & Settlements
 Baldur's Gate
 Moonshae Isles.
 Sumbria.
 Wa.
 Zakhara.

Trivia
The Red Larch had a sign hanging above its entrance depicting a piece of ham being cut by a cleaver.

## Clams in cream sauce

Clams or oysters in cream sauce was an elven seafood dish found in the Northwest Faerûn.

Description
This dish was made using oysters or clams that were steamed in beer. The cream sauce itself was a white condiment of dill, leeks, and chives. Traditional elven dishes were not made with onions or garlic as the Fair Folk consider their flavor to be overpowering and usually just reserved as "wayfaring staples" to mask the foul taste of rancid food in times of strife and struggle.

History
In the late 15 century DR, clams in the cream sauce were served among numerous other dishes at the resplendent mansion of a dragonborn wizard Torlinn Shrake, secretly a polymorphed beholder who usurped the mage's persona.

## Clever Jake

Clever Jake was a human adventurer and member of the Emerald Swords company. He was remarkable for his lack of redeeming qualities.

Personality
Clever Jake had an overinflated sense of himself, and thought himself quite clever. In actuality, he was extraordinarily dim and tended to annoy those around him.

Possessions
Clever Jake wore chain mail and carried a horseman's mace. He somehow possessed a helm of brilliance, which was the sole reason the Emerald Swords allowed him to stay in the group.

History
In 1369 DR, Clever Jake and the Emerald Swords were guests at Thistleflame Keep in Suzail. They conspired to kidnap Lord Partic Thistle and hand him over to Zhentil Keep for questioning, and had Xixis the doppelganger impersonate Lord Thistle to allow them to escape without suspicion. When Xixis risked carelessly ruining the plan, Gandegar Armstrong had Haridyn Storm kill Xixis with a dagger of venom, so that the Lord's murder would cover up his kidnapping. Despite their best attempts at diversion, the Emerald Swords were caught and Lord Thistle rescued. For their murder and kidnapping, they risked exile or execution for their crimes.

## Claylan

Count Claylan was nobleman of Amn in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Claylan was a pale human man who wore finery of black and green colors.

History
Count Claylan was a married man who spent his time seducing naive debutantes at the exclusive Mithrest inn located in the Waukeen's Promenade. Lady Lasalla was one of Claylan's conquests. He grew tired of the woman in a week, leaving her brokenhearted. She later felt annoyed at other debutantes who eagerly accepted the Count's advances. In the, Alicia Paalater found herself being courted by the Count and eagerly reciprocated. The young woman was enamored with the nobleman, praising his bravery after listening to the Count's tales and being impressed by his supposed influence over the Council of Six and Lord Ophal. He was planning to petition the Council to prohibit commoners from shopping at the Waukeen's Promenade during the day. Alicia was invited to visit Count Claylan's manor the same evening to view his collection of Sunite statuary. The nobleman insisted that her parents did not need to be distracted from their daily activities for a visit, especially since Claylan's wife was away from the manor.

## Cloak Bay

Cloak Bay, known as Raetheless among the Fair Folk, was an elongated bay just to the south of the Cloakwood on the Sword Coast North.

Description
The cliff shores of the Cloak Bay were sparsely populated, encircled by Cape Raeth to the west and the rocky beaches of the Jaws to the east. A smaller bay was connected to Raetheless just behind a diminutive peninsula known as the Fang. The smaller bay was called Borlyth Bay.

There were six settlements located around the Cloak Bay, but by the late 14 century DR, they were mostly abandoned because of increased attacks by the creatures of the Cloak Wood and pirate raids. Despite being sparsely populated, the villages were populated still by some hardy folk. These settlements were Orthul's Notch, Calyaun, Eldelorr, Sumbur Rock, Borlyth, and Ausabbason.

## Clipper

Clipper was a harengon who hailed from the Feywild. He was cursed to travel far and wide, and never allowed to settle down to a place he could call home.

Description
He resembled a large humanoid rabbit, an appearance he often had to refute to people unfamiliar with the harengon folk.

Personality

Clipper had an "act first, ask questions later" attitude and would readily throw himself into harm's way. He proved rather capable however, and got himself out of several precarious situations.

He was loyal to those that helped him, and would not leave them behind to fend for themselves.

Abilities

According to Clipper, he had a "nose for enchantment", and could sense things like curses and presumably magical dweomers.

Possessions
As an adventurer, he wielded a pair of handaxes.

Activities
Due to his curse, Clipper spent nearly all of his time traveling from one realm to another, unable to settle down at a single locale.

History

For a time, Clipper followed the Witchlight Carnival to the far-off world of Toril, where it stopped at the small town of Daggerford. There the intrepid harengon saw his opportunity to steal "magic words" from  the carriage of the carnival's proprietor, Mister Witch. He soon met a woman named Billi, who he sensed had also been afflicted with a "fey-born" curse. Clipper took Billi to the hall of mirrors, spoke the magic words, and transported himself and Billi to Thither, a realm in the demiplane of Prismeer.

Clipper and Billi got into trouble almost immediately after entering into Thither. The pair stole an owlbear-led chariot from an elf man, and rammed it into Honeyhollow Fortress, an outpost for a community of sprites. They were then taken captive by the tiny, militaristic fey and forced to make a bargain: in exchange for their freedom, Clipper and Billi would break into the lair of the hag Granny Skabatha Nightshade, and steal valuable spell components for the sprites. This was a fortunate turn of events for Clipper's companion. It had just become apparent to Billi that Granny Nightshade was the monster responsible for abducting her brother Desmond some eight years prior.

The two broke into Granny Nightshade's lair and, with the help of the hag's apprentice Mishka, overcame her fey minions. During the fracas, Clipper got into a battle with green dragon wyrmling, before meeting the hag herself. Granny Nightshade offered to free Clipper from his curse, provided he join her forces. Clipper refused and stayed true to his new friend. Fortunately for the due, Billi drew up the answer to Granny Nightshade's riddle, and freed her brother from the hag's clutches. Clipper, Billi, and Desmond were instantaneously banished from the Feywild and sent back to the world of Toril.

Not content to merely live their lives as before their encounter with Granny Nightshade, Clipper and Billi decided they would return to Prismeer, and free Mishka and other children like Desmond who had been taken captive by the malevolent hag.

## Cloudspore mushroom

Cloudspore mushrooms were a peculiar species of fungus that typically grew in the Underdark.

Description
This species of mushroom grew in patches and was so named for its propensity to unleash thick clouds of spores when disturbed. Once a cloud was discharged, a patch of cloudspores would not unleash another for 24 hours.

Habitats
 Beyond the Prime Material plane, it was not uncommon to encounter cloudspores in Molor, the 528 layer of the Abyss ruled by Juiblex.

History
At some point long before the 15 century DR, a Silvanus worshiping druid took up residence within a barrow in southern Unther and therein cultivated cloudspore mushrooms. Centuries later, when a young blue dragon took up residence in the abandoned barrow in 1479 DR, a few of the druid's specimens still remained.

## Cloudspire

Cloudspire was the tallest mountain on all of Toril. It was located in the central Yehimal Mountains of western Kara-Tur, south of the Kun-Yen Shan range.Ed Greenwood via Candlekeep

Geography
Cloudspire was located in the central highlands of the Yehimal, just to the west of the immense glacier on which sat the spelljamming port known as the Dock. It was south of the Kun-Yen Shan, north-west of the Po Yul Dzayul Range, and west of M'tsin Gompa in Tabot.

The mountain had little mineral wealth and several interior caverns, which were once home to dragons but were later the lair of remorhaz.

The mountain's precise height was unknown, but it was estimated at between 35,000 and 36,000 feet (10,700 to 10,900 meters).For comparison, this mountain is taller than Mount Everest, at 29,029 ft (8,848 meters).

Etymology
The mountain was known by many names to various species. The elves called it Arthael, whilst the dwarves referred to it as Yauvdagh. The gnomes called it Yavalaur. Human names for the mountain included Dzayur, Mount Huul, and Kulmont.

## Cledwyll

Cledwyll was a renowned Cormyte sculptor who lived sometime during the 10 century DR.

History
Cledwyll's original art was worth a fortune and had a very distinct style.

One of his pieces, a curvaceous sculpture of the goddess Selûne caved out of black stone, was personally gifted by Cledwyll to the founder of the noble House Wyvernspur of Cormyr, Paton Wyvernspur, sometime before the Lord's death in the . Cledwyll's Selûne was almost  tall and  around. The sculpture was graceful and scantily clad; the woman's position was arched with arms raised up over her head in a circle, her lips slightly parted, with her eyes closed as if waiting for a surprise. The statue eventually came to be stored in Drone Wyvernspur's laboratory in the Redstone Castle in the mid–14 century DR, wrapped in red velvet and twine. Drone described Cledwyll's Selûne as ample, while the family's stern matriarch Dorath Wyvernspur called the statue scandalous. After his supposed death, Drone sent Cledwyll's Selûne, the patron goddess of the family, to his nephew Giogioni Wyvernspur as a hint at the family's history. Later, a Harper "bard" named Olive Ruskettle instantly recognized the black marble salacious statue as one of Cledwyll's originals.

Shortly before the, another one of Cledwyll's originals was purchased by Jannaxil Serpentil of Waterdeep. The purchase was detailed in his account book, which was stolen by Harpers Danilo Thann and Arilyn Moonblade.

## Clay

Clay was a common naturally-occurring kind of soil in Faerûn and beyond, often used to make useful and beautiful ceramic objects.

Description

Clay resembled thick mud or soft stone depending on how dry it was, and weighed between  and  per . It was a particularly difficult kind of soil to handle with tools, with even hoes and plows struggling to break through the stiff and heavy mass.

Availability
High-quality raw clay for use in magical spells and rituals was available for purchase through the Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue for 1 gold piece per . Another variety was available for only 1 silver piece per pound, as well as  fired spheres for 1 copper piece.

Clay Deposits in the Realms
 In the Abyss, the cavernous Worm Realm was made up of earth, stone, and clay.
 In the Delimbiyr Vale, there were rich clay pits along the banks of the River Shining.
 Around Corm Orp.
 The Elemental Plane of Earth was made up of various minerals, stones, and soils, with clays included.
 The northern reaches of the Stonelands were largely made up of dry soil unsuited to agriculture.

Usage

 Clay was commonly fired in kilns to make ceramic objects such as crockery, jars, sculptures, and tiles, in varieties such as stoneware, earthenware, terracotta, and porcelain.
 Clay was used by scholars such as alchemists and wizards in their experiments, as it was a nonreactive substance that could be shaped as desired. Moist clay could be used to make heat-absorbing pads for holding hot vessels, shaped into temporary joins for glass piping, or moulded onto skin to protect against heat and acid.
 Protective gear such as canvas aprons and gloves were steeped in clay, making them extra effective against acids, poisons, heat, and other hazards. Canvas sailcloth for ships could also be imbued with clay to make it more resistant to wind, water, salt, and other threats to their integrity.
 Clay could be used as part of disguise kits to alter facial features.
 Clay golems were among the cheaper and simpler golems that could be made.
 On the island nation of Wa, clay was one of the two common materials used in the construction of buildings.
Food
 Fensir were known to eat some forms of clay.
 Magma beasts subsisted on clay, among other things.
Spell Components
 Conjure elemental required a small amount of soft clay if the caster wanted to use it to conjure an earth elemental.
 Distance distortion used a small amount of soft clay.
 Earthquake used a lump of clay.
 Maximilian's earthen grasp required a miniature hand sculpted from clay.
 Move earth used a bag of clay, loam, and sand mixed together.
 Rising colossus used a ball of clay large enough to fill a caster's hand.
 Stone shape used a piece of clay shaped into the desired form.
 Transmute rock to mud used some clay and water.

Magic
 Move earth could shift a large volume of clay and other soils.
 Soften earth and stone turned an area of stone into a soft clay.

The cloak of the Weave was a magical, protective cloak that allowed the wearer to absorb and harness elemental energy.

Twice per day, the cloak of the Weave could be used to absorb elemental energy and partially negate any harm they suffered from its effect. That energy was then redirected to the wearer's own powers, and allowed them to inflict a portion of that energy against foes during any next attack they made.

Additionally, the cloak granted the wearer inherent protection against magical spells and their effects.

## Clyssavar

Clyssavar was a gold dragon who was mentioned in legends about Stormwreck Isle.

History
Clyssavar was one of the three great metallic dragons who defeated the great red dragon Sharruth. Legends say that he, the bronze dragon Astalagan, and the brass dragon Turadaer joined forces to battle Sharruth and confined her beneath the ocean floor.

It is unknown how exactly Clyssavar died, but it was centuries prior to the late 1400s DR and it was "a violent death". His bones could be found scattered across the rocky shoal on the north side of Stormwreck Isle. Through the centuries, this site caused many shipwrecks including the wreck of the Compass Rose

As of the, a blue dragon wyrmling named Sparkrender had taken up residence in the Clifftop Observatory on Stormwreck Isle and attempted to harness the power of the island's fallen dragons. For this cause, he ordered his kobold followers Mek and Minn to build and paint sculptures of the five great dragons, Clyssavar among them. When a group of adventurers attempted to stop the wyrmling's ritual, the effigy of Clyssavar provided the power of the long dead dragon, unleashing 'Clyssavar's Flames' in glowing golden colors upon Sparkrender.

## Cloud shape

Cloud shape was an alteration spell, developed by the air elementalist Mortimer Lightfoot in the mid–14 century DR, that allowed one to manipulate the shape of clouds.

Effects
One or more clouds within a  radius could be manipulated by the caster into limitless number of shapes for an unlimited duration. The spell also allowed the caster to "stretch" clouds out to twice their original size and join multiple clouds together. The inherent nature of the cloud would be left unaffected.

Components
In addition to requiring somatic and verbal components, cloud shape required a material component, which took the form of the cloud that was to be used by the spell.

History
In the mid–14 century DR, Mortimer Lightfoot and a group of other rogue elementalists known as the Circle of Four used cloud shape, alongside Mortimer's other spell solid cloud, to create a sort of cloud palace that they used to imprison their kidnapped victims.

## Clockwork toy

Clockwork toys, sometimes known simply as clockworks, were small delicate mechanical figures created for recreational means. They were typically created by members of the Church of Gond, though some of the industrious rock gnomes of Toril were also known to craft them, as were the tinker gnomes of Krynn.

Description
Clockwork toys were created in a variety of different forms. Many were crafted in the shape of animals, such as birds, fish, frogs, or mice. But others were crafted in the forms of humans or demihumans, dragons, soldiers, or various types of monsters, such as cockatrices.

They were made of metal, such as brass or gold. And their delicate shells were crammed full of springs, wires, and other clockwork that enabled them to walk, fly, or swim for short distances. Some even emitted imitations of the sounds of creatures they were crafted to resemble.

Some clockwork toys took on more esoteric forms. One such example from the late 15 century DR was that of egg-shaped jewelry boxes, which had several layers that rotated within each other, and played tunes like a music box. Through their revolutions these layers would reveal elaborated etched or painted scenes. Commonly depicted scenes included such things as royal feasts, men dueling with swords, or skeletons within a dungeon escaping from the confines of their shackles.

Availability
 A great deal of the Church of Gond's temples were known to sell clockwork toys, typically at high prices, by the late 15 century DR.
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold a wide variety of Gondsmen crafted clockwork toys imported from Lantan and tested thoroughly by their salespeople to ensure each toy functioned properly, though the catalogue claimed that this was also because the salespeople themselves enjoyed playing with them. These toys measured  in length and were shaped like mundane animals. Their metal bodies were notably painted and draped with streamers. The catalogue sold these individually for 2 gold pieces a piece, but also offered buying bundles at a price of 10 or 15 gold pieces.
 Bunsen’s Clockwork Shop, a small shop in the heart of Ravens Bluff's Merchant District operated by the Gondar priest Father Bunsen, carried hundreds of ornate brass and gold clockwork toys. Kept well polished, these toys lined the walls of Bunsen's shop from within display shelves. In addition to animals, they took the forms of dragons, dwarves, elves, soldiers, and a wide array of monsters.
 Firestorm House, one of the Merchant Houses of Ravens Bluff, sold clockwork toys throughout the Eastern Heartlands.
 The House of Cloevaerus, a mercantile company in Ravens Bluff that imported clockworks and similar contraptions, through struggled under the fierce competition of Firestorm House.
 Thimblewine's Pawnshop, a shop in Waterdeep operated by the rock gnome Krystaleen, sold clockwork toys for 50 gp a piece.
 The Toysmiths, a toyshop in Ravens Bluff that was owned and operated by the gnome brothers Gunder and Relvan Gaewilder, sold intricate clockwork toys that they sold at a high prices  to Sembians and Waterdhavians. These toys took the form of life-like birds or other small animals, crafted from gold and with gems for eyes.
 In the land of Zakhara, clockwork toys were commonly created by clockwork mages.

History
Prior to the Spellplague era, the Lantan's supply and sale of such contraptions on mainland Faerûn was more limited in scope.

For a time it was considered quite fashionable among Cormyrean nobles to gift King Azoun Obarskyr IV one of the intricate clockwork toys crafted by the Gaewilder brothers of Ravens Bluff, until Azoun himself grew tiresome of the fad and would sigh loudly whenever he was presented with another.

In the late 14 century DR, after a consortium of Lantanese gnome-ran merchant organizations established the Lantan-Samarach portal, the gnomes provided the local Samarachans new methods of illusory defenses and the wonders of Lantanese clockwork toys.

Notable Owners
 The Ravenian merchant Bendekar kept a collection of clockwork toys among the hoards of miscellaneous goods he kept in his residence.
 Einrich Thomas, a Gondar priest and member of The Turning Wheel clergy in Ravens Bluff, devoted much of his time to creating clockwork toys for children and over time had created roughly thousands of them. Whenever Winterfest came around, he would go from door to door in the city passing out his creations to the Ravenian children.
 The Falinescu family of Barovia owned some clockwork toys.
 The Industrious Brothers and Sisters of Carpentry, Cabinetry, Puppetry, and Toymaking.While it is not detailed what types of crafts this religious ordered devoted themselves to, outside of those listed in its name, it can be inferred that clockwork toys were most likely part of their repertoire on account of Gondsmen-made clockwork toys being widely documented across other sources.

## Cloak of plenty

Cloaks of plenty were enchanted cloaks that protected, warmed, and fed their wearers, created sometime in the 14 century DR.

Description
A cloak of plenty was a quality cloak made of black wool and signed with deep purple satin.

Powers
When examined with detect magic, these cloaks radiated a palpable alteration magic aura. When worn, a cloak of plenty offered significant protection against elemental effects and magic while always keeping the wearer warm and dry. Its magical comfort was such that the wearer could sleep on a pile of rocks and still wake up rested and healed. It also granted the wearer the boon of ultravision.

The cloak's lining also hid twelve pockets filled with extra-dimensional spaces. Each pocket and each space held one of the following things: half a pound (230 grams) of cheese, half a pound of warm fresh cinnamon raisin bread, one pound of spare ribs, four ears of steamed sweet corn smothered in butter, one apple, one orange, one pear, half a pint (280 milliliters) of honey, half a pound of cracked walnuts, one quart of strawberries, one quart of cold apple cider, one quart chilled drinking water, and a single hunting knife. The pockets' contents were refilled through the cloak's magics daily.

History
In the mid–14 century DR, a cloak of plenty belonged to Lady Holly Graham who ran Ravens Bluff's only cider mill.

## Cloud ray

Cloud rays were a species of gargantuan, manta ray-shaped magical beasts of primeval origins, with a connection to earth, that flew through the skies of the desert world of Athas.

Description
These creatures had broad, flattened, and triangular bodies with long pectoral fins on each side. A bulge ran the length of their spine, with it and the abdomen tapering off into a long, thin, whip-like tail. This appendage, referred to as the "zip", was quite agile and compromised of very sharp, barbed ridges that grew at the tip of the creature's spine. They had four eyes of jet black hue, one on each side of their snout and cranial bulge, and were guarded from harm by sets of ridges.

Their bodies were enormous in scope, measured by some to achieve sizes of  by, tails measuring, and mouths that on average measured  across. These gaping maws were capable of swallowing whole prey as large as,In Monstrous Compendium - Dark Sun, it is stated that cloud rays can swallow whole anything smaller than size "huge", while in Monstrous Compendium II for 3 edition it is stated they can swallow a creature that is "Huge or smaller". In 2 edition, size "huge" creatures were classified as anything within the range of, while in 3 and 3.5 a size "huge" creature was classified as anything between . and emptied all food directly into the creature's intestines. Lacking any appreciable true stomach, their interior jaw ridges and long throats were lined with teeth designed for vigorously grinding and tearing its food down to a near liquid consistency.

The colors of these vast creatures were mottled in shades of either speckled-brown, dark blue, or gray. Their undersides displayed similar, though lighter shades of those colors, usually being a drab olive and white. Juvenile cloud rays by contrast had a translucent quality to their skin and were relatively fragile.

Behavior
Cloud rays were generally docile unless disturbed, most often attacking simply due to hunger. In particular, attempting to contact cloud rays psionically would throw them into a horrible rage, driving them to madly pursue the devour the psionicist responsible. Though they would go to great lengths to capture the offender, they would never resort to landing.

Cloud rays were also a territorial species. They respected the "air space" of one another and, with few exceptions, would not intrude upon the territory of another cloud ray.

Abilities
Cloud rays were an innately psionic species. They possessed the discipline of psychometabolism, having under it the psionic science of telekinesis, as well as the psionic devotions control winds, inertial barrier, and levitation.To different degrees, these psionic abilities are retained in the 3 edition iteration of cloud rays. It is detailed in Monster Manual II as having innate levitation and telekinesis abilities, similar to the spells of the same name, whilst inertial barrier is simplified down to an innate ability that functions like the spell protection from arrows.

They were capable of a sort of "flight", by means of a combination of their innate psionic power of levitation and lighter-than-air gases they produced and manipulated inside of their bodies. Their bodies were further propelled and maneuvered by flapping their enormous pectoral fins.

Cloud rays also possessed a single power under the psionic discipline psychoportation known as dream travel. Cloud rays sometimes tapped into this power while digesting a good meal or sleeping, though would more consciously utilize it when injured.

Combat
The enormous stature of cloud rays posed a great threat to the people of Athas even when the creatures were not outwardly trying to harm, as the flapping of their pectoral fins could kick up sand like a deadly sandstorm and decimate entire villages in their wake.

When hunting for prey, cloud rays typically resorted straight to snatching up and swallowing their prey whole. On some occasions they might drop snatch prey to the ground from high in the sky in an effort to soften them up. It was whenever this "swallow whole" maneuver failed that a cloud ray would attempt to get as close as possible to a prey and strike with their tail in a whip-like fashion, then if possible turn themselves around to follow that attack up with a bite. This tail lash happened at an incredible speed, producing a thunderous clap in the process. And just a few strikes of their tail was powerful enough to destroy most structures.

Cloud ray pups primarily fought by flitting around their prey and repeatedly stinging them with their tail.

Ecology
Cloud rays lived solitary lives, drifting aimlessly through the skies of Athas, always in search of their next meal. They were ocassionally seen in pairs, though this was only during times of mating.

Cloud rays gestated their young over a period of three years. When it came time to birth their young, the mother would take to the skies and do so while airborne, leaving the male to catch them on his back. These adolescents, commonly referred to as "pups", would be raised by their parents for the first year of their life. During this time they would be supported on the male's back, whilst the female of the pair foraged for food. Once a cloud ray pup learned how to fly and was capable of being self-sufficient, the family would part ways. Once left to themselves, cloud ray pups tended to either follow other predators in hopes of scavenging their scraps or gather around a creature that would provide food to them, with this usually being an elder cloud ray.

On rare ocassions, elder cloud rays would descend and settle themselves down on the surface when they needed to fall into a deep slumber, often covering their bodies in sand and other nearby debris through a combination of their psionic telekinesis and flapping their pectoral fins. In this camouflaged state they could often be mistaken for rock outcroppings, with some stories telling of travelers camping only to awaken the next day to find themselves either somewhere else or far above in the sky. This state of slumber would last until either the cloud ray grew hungry or it experienced some external disturbance.

Diet
Cloud rays subsisted primarily upon a mixture of magical, psychic, and entropic energies. This was supplemented by a carnivorous, with the amount of physical sustenance needed becoming less as they grew older. They preferred to prey upon other flying creatures, typically being fond of rocs and flying humanoids.
Languages
Being creatures of an animal intellect, cloud rays lacked a sophisticated language of their own, though they did have a complex system of signalling to one another that utilized their tail.
Usages
If by some means a cloud ray was ever successfully slain, its enormous body could provide two to three months worth of raw materials and meat to a settlement.

History
On Toril, in The Year of Despair, Malatran calendar, late 14 century DR, a group of heroes of the Malatran Plateau were sent through an elemental gate to the far-away world of Athas. Created temporarily by Roshink, the elemental noble explained that he and his fellow nobles fled from this world long ago and it is what could befall the Malatran Plateau should the heroes fail to vanquish the dreaded Tamara. In order to return home, he instructed them to head for the mountains until they came across a great forest, where they would find an "oak tree that is too big for a saru to put his arms around". When they found such an oak, he would be able to bring them back to the Plateau through the Dreamlands. Immediately upon arrival, the group was captured by Athasian elf slavers. A young thri-kreen named Ka'cha was among creatures captured by the elves, and he lamented being separated from his clutch. Subsequently, the heroes escaped when the elven caravan was attacked by a hungry sink worm. They seized the opportunity and freed Ka'cha, fleeing from their pursuers across the desert.

After two days of travel across the hot sands, guided by Ka'cha, the group stumbled across his tribe, the Windrunners, and their leader, Ki'rik'ku. Ka'cha convinced them that the strange adventurers were friends, not food. Happy with one of their number returned, Chief Ki'rik'ku gifted the aliens four large erdlu eggs and two apples of healing. The thri-kreen then explained to the Malatran heroes how to reach their destination–the Ringing Forest–the way back to Toril.

While traveling to the forest through a path in the Ringing Mountains, the Malatran heroes found themselves under attack by a particularly hungry cloud ray.

## Clan Eaglecleft

Clan Eaglecleft was one of the dwarven clans of Faerûn's northern reaches in the 14 and 15 century DR.

Appendix
Background
In the Living City tournament adventure Dwarf in the Fold, designer David Santana included within DM material a section detailing some of the various dwarven clans of the Vast (a region of North Faerûn) that had appeared in various other Living City material. There he states, "This material draws upon official sources, but much of it comes from my imagination. I have tried to denote what is official and what I created." This section has him suggesting that Jalagalar Forkbeard is a member of Clan Eaglecleft. Jalagar's first and only other mention is in The City of Ravens Bluff, where he is stated to have been one of eight Master Masons of ancient Sarbreen.

Due to David's phrasing, the fact that neither Jalagalar Forkbeard nor Clan Eaglecleft ever appeared or were mentioned in any subsequent Living City material, and the wiki's Canon Policy Hierarchy ranking licensed material (such as tournament adventures) below sourcebooks – it is very unlikely that Eaglecleft has any relation to Sarbreen and the Vast.

The assassins were defeated, leaving behind a damaged Clan Solar'el dagger that may had eventually been reforged by a master from Luskan, using shards of the similar weapons found in the lair of Rhylfang.

## Closeguard Island

Closeguard Island, also called Closeguard Isle or simply Closeguard, was an island that sat in the mouth of the River Mirar in the city of Luskan. It was notable as the headquarters of the High Captain of Ship Kurth.

Description
As headquarters of Ship Kurth, the main landmark on this rocky island was Kurth Tower, and only members of Ship Kurth or of the Arcane Brotherhood were allowed on the island. The island also included some barracks and training facilities as well as a number of residences.

Belowground, partially flooded tunnels infested with undead connected Closeguard Island to crypts beneath the Ruins of Illusk, and from there, to a network of sewers and tunnels throughout Luskan that led as far down as the Underdark. A hidden underwater cave below the western rim of Closeguard led directly into these underground tunnels.

Geography
Closeguard Island was one of five islands in Luskan (the others being Blood Island, Cutlass Island, Fang Island, and Harbor Arm Island). Closeguard lay between Cutlass Island to the east and the Ruins of Illusk on Luskan's South Bank to the west. It was connected to Cutlass Island via the Sword Bridge and to the South Bank via the Dark Arch bridge. It sat just upstream of Dragon Beach and just downstream of Blood Island, although it was not connected to either of these.

Defenses
While the island was protected by guards as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR, it was unguarded by the late 15 century DR, however anyone who was not a known member of Ship Kurth or the Arcane Brotherhood was quickly singled out regardless. The island was protected via a ward placed on the Dark Arch which alerted the Arcane Brotherhood if anyone not affiliated with them were to cross it. Sword Bridge, in turn, was defended by battle horrors.

History
During the mid–1360s DR, the Archmage Arcane Arklem Greeth personally strengthened the magical defenses of the island, particularly those of the Dark Arch bridge and of Kurth Tower.

In the, when Deudermont led an effort to unseat Arklem Greeth and break the Arcane Brotherhood's grip on Luskan, Closeguard Island became a critical strategic position as it controlled access to the Brotherhood's Host Tower of the Arcane on Cutlass Island. While High Captain Kurth tentatively supported Deudermont, he had his men blockaded both the east and west bridges of Closeguard, which trapped the Brotherhood's wizards on Cutlass Island but also forced Deudermont to attack the Host Tower by sea. Kurth was fearful of the chaos that would ensue if he committed to one side or the other, which would push the other High Captains to take sides as well, however he eventually sided with Deudermont and allowed his troops to cross the island. At the conclusion of the battle, when Greeth caused an explosion that destroyed the Host Tower, the blast was destructive enough to incinerate a number of homes on the western shore of Closeguard.

Following the downfall of the Arcane Brotherhood, Ship Kurth fortified Closeguard Island as the city of Luskan descended into chaos. A number of the Brotherhood's survivors took up residence on the island at that time, under the protection of Kurth.

By, Captain Borlann of Ship Rethnor had taken up residence on the island.

In the, Closeguard Island was assailed by a horde of demons, gnolls, goblins, ogres, and pirates as part of House Margaster's invasion of Luskan. While some of these invaders laid siege to Kurth Tower, others crossed the island to attack Cutlass Island.

Notable Locations
 Kurth Tower, the fortress residence of High Captain Kurth.
 The Crow's Nest, a thin stone tower built by Borlann of Ship Rethnor in the mid–15 century DR.
 A fortified "guesthouse" for prisoners of the Arcane Brotherhood. Such prisoners were either kept under guard in the house or chained in the dungeons beneath it.

## Clockwork prosthetic

Clockwork prosthetics were mechanical prosthetics.

Description
Clockwork prosthetics were highly specialized and costly, with prices starting at 10,000 gold pieces for a leg, 20,000 for an arm, and around 7,500 for a hand, owing to its complex parts. The production process for each prosthetic was labor-intensive, requiring a month for design and an additional one to three months to construct and refine each limb.

Clockwork prosthetics offered durability and strength that natural limbs could not. Prosthetic arms and hands inflicted additional damage while unarmed, while the prosthetic legs enabled powerful kicks. The durability of each limb varied.

Despite their remarkable functionality, clockwork prosthetics had inherent limitations. Regular maintenance was essential, as they must be oiled frequently to prevent jamming or corrosion. Additionally, they required winding several times per day, with each winding powering the limb for a few hours. They could not be wound continuously to last an entire day.

The mechanical nature of these limbs reduced their effectiveness compared to natural limbs. Prosthetic legs, for instance, decreased movement speed, and prosthetic arms and hands diminished dexterity for ranged attacks and had a negative effect on skills such as picking pockets, unlocking mechanisms, and disarming traps. All clockwork prosthetics produced a faint whirring sound, which made it difficult to be stealthy.

The creators of clockwork prosthetics,Tohkkal and Tykkyl Burrwarden, offered customizable upgrades at additional cost. Optional features included increased durability and concealed spring-loaded weaponry, although such modifications typically more than doubled the initial cost.

## Clan Daeronnax

Clan Daeronnax was a group of kobolds that served the great red dragon Daeronnax in the Stonelands during the late 15 century DR.

History
The clan lived around the lava-filled lair of the wyrm Daeronnax and patrolled the tunnels nearby. During the 1480s DR, an ancient Netherese tomb was discovered by a team of adventurers made up of Jarus Brithandir, Helayna, Tristran of Daggerford, Kirina Aelorothi, Liantor Stoneborn, and Popkin Shortshanks. They passed through the upper levels and blundered into the lower sections controlled by Clan Daeronnax. The party was overcome by an ambush that isolated and scattered them, with only Popkin escaping the caves entirely. The other remained lost in the underground, with Liantor killed and Tristran captured and delivered as a prize to their draconic master. The reinforcements that Popkin brought to bear may have saved the adventurers and slain the dragon, potentially destroying the clan as well.

## Clymph Tower

Clymph Tower was a long abandoned keep in eastern Dunwood in the Great Dale.

History
The Nar demonbinder Clymph built the proud walled keep of Clymph Tower in the, as a pen for his foul minions, the demons that he had summoned and bound to his will. Imbuing the very stones with abjurative magic, he made the entire perimeter of the keep into a great magic circle from which the demons could not escape out into the wilderness. Clymph dreamt of founding a mighty empire in the Great Dale with his demonic armies, and created a permanent gate to the Abyss from which he drew his forces.

Two years later in  he created Tower Threespires, and linked the two by a permanent portal, so as to be able to come and leave as he pleased, while bypassing the magic circle of Clymph Tower. Unfortunately for Clymph and his ambitions, he was unable to control the demonic forces that he had summoned, and he was slain by the very instrument of conquest he had made. The demons, for their part, were unable to leave the keep as the demon-trap was so well made, and the portal through to Tower Threespires was sealed.

In the centuries since, those few who approached the lonesome keep reported that its stones glowed red with an inner heat, and the sound of a massive fire was to be heard within.

In the  a band of adventurers ventured into the keep, aiming to banish the demons that still lingered, but they were never seen nor heard from again.

During the Spellplague after 1385 DR, the gate to the Abyss was torn asunder and many of the imprisoned demons were succumbed from the lack of energy. Yet near a century later, a fiendish presence still lingered upon the ruins.

Rumors & Legends
In the  a legend spoke of a booming voice greeting anyone who knocked thrice on the gate of the keep, and every so often they would receive an answer to a single question, spoken so quietly that one would need to press one's ear to the gate to hear the answer. Although most learned what they desired and departed, a few others would instead be absorbed into the very gate they pressed their head to, swallowed up by the keep and never to be heard from again. Nevertheless, it had a reputation as a dark oracle.

## Clarkswell

Clarkswell was a small farming town in northern Cormyr.

Description
Clarkswell laid fifty miles north of Arabel, in the foothills of the Storm Horn Mountains, and sustained itself on farming. Clarkswell was a small town of only several businesses and little trade to speak of.

The Town Square laid at the village's center, on Market Way. A smithy, stables, and a general store surrounded the Town Square. The Cooked Goose Inn stood on the crossing of Market Way and Lynway, just to the north, facing the Town Square. Further north, the next block was separated by Merdocks Street. This was where the leather goods store was found with the mage protector's tower across the street crossing. One block south from the Town Square stood the town's guardhouse and the mayor's manse.

Government
The town was governed by a mayor. Two other officials were the protector mage and the guard captain. Gladgo Haltok, a retired adventurer, was the kindly, well-liked lord-mayor in the mid-14 century DR. During the same time period, the title of the guard captain was held by Lyle Grobit, an immensely strong but moody fighter. And lastly, Eloras was the town's introverted protector mage.

Defenses
Under guard captain Lyle Grobit's leadership, the town's guard company consisted of forty fighters and sixty trained civilian militiamen. Twenty of the guards were usually on patrol at the time.

History
Protector mage Eloras saved Clarkswell from monsters on three separate occasions before or in the mid-14 century DR. In the mid-14 century DR, a farmstead ten miles north of town was hit by a hill giant raid. Another event of note was a robbery of the lord-mayor's manse and the mage protector's tower by a group of adventurers who pretended to be members of the nobility but, in truth, sought an acceptance into Brandon's Band of Thieves of Arabel.

Notable Locations
 Blacksmith's shop overlooking the Town Square, run by Mercer Strongson in the mid-14 century DR.
 Cooked Goose Inn, the only inn in town of note, managed by Markel Harkwhite in the mid-14 century DR. The tavern served travelers, adventurers, merchants, and farmers and offered free lodgings and meals to visiting bards. Markel's wife was the inn's cook and theorist two daughters, Sinali Harkwhite and Talli Harkwhite, worked as servers.
 General store, owned by Mitzi Elder, an eagle-eyed and pleasant woman, in the mid-14 century DR.
 Leather Goods for All, a leatherworker's workshop and store run by Jason Hetrick in the mid-14 century DR.
 Mayor's house, a large mansion, the biggest building in town.
 Stables on the Town Square, run by Norcal, an avid smoker, in the mid-14 century DR.
 Wizard's tower, home of Eloras, the protector mage, consisted of only two rooms – a workroom on the first floor and the upper floor bedroom. His grandson's home stood across the street from the tower.

Notable Inhabitants
 Brazee, a neurotic farmer who frequented the Cooked Goose Inn in the mid-14 century DR.
 Climm, a jack of all trades and a Cooked Goose's regular patron in the mid-14 century DR.
 Gutrock Trollbane, an adventurer visitor to Clarkswell.
 Josiah Crimple, a happy-go-lucky farmer and Brazee's dining companion in the mid-14 century DR.
 Norf, a Cooked Goose's regular patron in the mid-14 century DR.
 Paul Portis, a hired farm laborer in the mid-14 century DR.
 Xanthes Smithson, a traveling merchant who dealt in arms and armor, a regular business visitor to Clarkswell.
