## Clarorna Amaerityl

Clarorna Amaerityl, the so-called Wizardess of Athkatla, was a mage for hire based in Amn and often employed by House Selemchant in the late 15 century DR.

Personality
In her old age, Clarorna's body was constantly in aches of aging that defied her life-prolonging magics. The constant pain made her temper shorter than it once was. Nevertheless, she was a woman with a sharp mind who showed kindness to the common folk and demanded the nobility and the wealthy to act with respect and responsibility.

Abilities
As a wizard, she was a force to be reckoned with. When things turned to violence, Clarorna was well-protected by a chain of contingency spells that released up to six "hanging" spells that were ready to be triggered to teleport her away and dispense magical healing, as well as leaving behind parting gifts of deadly offensive incantations.

Activities
Even though Clarorna appeared to be around forty-eight years old, she was much older in reality—more than ninety years old, in fact. As a researcher who studied longevity that did not involve lichdom, she managed to preserve her looks. Still, the age and frailty nevertheless began affecting her body with aches and pains, which affected her temper.

Relationships
Clarorna Amaerityl maintained a relationship with the Council of Five of Amn that allowed her to voice her opinions bluntly and directly when she thought their policies to be barbaric or lacking foresight.

Clarorna Amaerityl was a close friend of Laeral Silverhand, the Lady Mage and the Open Lord of Waterdeep. However, Alustriel Silverhand and Clarorna shared no love and were politely cool with each other at best. Elminster and Clarorna shared a relationship of jests and jabs, as she often called the Old Mage a useful "loose cannon."

History
By the late 15 century DR, Clarorna Amaerityl had made a name for herself by stepping into situations where mercantile resistance turned into violence.

## Cloak of Mystra

Cloaks of Mystra, more colloquially known as Mystra's Blessing, were enchanted cloaks created by the Church of Mystra to offer mages and sorcerers of the land additional protection against harm.

Powers
Each cloak of Mystra was blessed by the Mother of All Magic to grant the wearer resistance to damage done by physical means. The cloaks protected their wearers as if bearing a simple +1 enchantment, but, in addition to that protection, Mystra's blessing allowed the cloaks to absorb some of the damage done. Due to the nature of the item, such cloak could only be worn by a wizard or a sorcerer.

History
Cloaks of Mystra were first created sometime before the . In the, one such cloak belong to the proprietor of Icewind Enchantments in Targos, Silvenius Crim, a member of the Arcane Brotherhood, sent to establish operations in Icewind Dale.

## Clove

Cloves were a type of spice in the Realms noted for their aromatic nature.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold cloves across the reaches of Faerûn under its "Aurora's Larder" and "Priests' Alcove" sections.
 In the Thayan port city of Priador, one could commonly find imported cloves for sale in the Priador Emporium's spice shop.
 On the island nation of Wa, the port city of Akkaido often received shipments of cloves.
 Cloves were cultivated in some parts of Zakhara, such as the city-state of Huzuz.

Price
The price of cloves somewhat varied in Faerûn. Whilst Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold cloves for use by priests at a rate of 1 silver piece per, the cloves sold for culinary use under "Aurora's Larder" were priced at 35 gp per .

Over in the land of Zakhara, a  chest of of cloves would be valued at 400 gold pieces.

Usage
Food & Drink
 Across the various higher-class inns, taverns, and other eateries in the Realms, the "First Meat" course in an eveningfeast meal was typically garnished with hard-boiled eggs that had been spiced with cloves.
 On the Malatran Plateau, cloves were used as a pickling spice, together with allspice, mustard seeds, black peppercorns, red chiles, coriander, ginger, and mace.
 Bowl of hares, the specialty dish of the Oak-Father's Boon, used cloves in its rich sauce.
 Fowl stew, a poultry dish from the Singing Sprite inn of Secomber, was flavored with a variety of spices, including a pinch of either cinnamon or ground cloves.
 Gravia's hot apple cider included two whole cloves as part of its recipe.
 Roast gammon pie, a Cormyrian dish, called for a finger of ground cloves, or in other words a heap just large enough to stay on a cook's finger.
 A typical recipe for mulled brew included whole cloves.
 Cloves were often a spice of choice when making Shoodra sauce.
 The spiced wine of Calimshan and Tethyr were flavored with a variety of herbs, spices, and fruits, including cloves. A specifically clove flavored wine could be ordered through Aurora's.
 Stag sausage, a prized meat good produced by Wyrmkindler Sausage Works, called for 1 thruppinch of cloves.
 Suulvyk, a rare hard-boiled egg in drow cuisine that was traditionally flavored with cloves imported from the World Above.
 Vilhon Cider was often said to be enhanced by the addition of cloves.

Other
 Due to their aromatic nature, cloves were sometimes used in incense, scented oils, perfumes, and pipeweed.
 Cloves had a wide array of medicinal uses, including as anesthetic, disinfectant, germicide, and aiding in circulation.

Trivia
 In the land of Zakhara, the fragrance-loving great ghuls and sakinas were known to delight in the aroma of cloves.

## Claive the Carver

Claive the Carver was a Cormyrean bandit who prowled the King's Forest in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Claive was a hulkingly large man who wore a half-mask over his face and carried a sword.

History
During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Claive was part of a werewolf-filled bandit gang that predated upon the King's Forest and High Road. With the collapse of Cormyrean authority in the region due to the war, the bandits went largely uncontested as they raided and robbed. When the drow destroyed a Purple Dragons forest outpost the bandits moved in to use it as their own base, not realizing that it was an important magical site. In response, the Wizard of War Arganon Pine struck back with the Purple Dragons, intent on completing a ritual that would protect the area from dark influences. Claive joined the bandits' counterattack on the Cormyreans, but fell in battle alongside the werewolf Bladeback.

## Clarrisa

Clarrisa was a bowyer and fletcher in Stag Falls during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
Darrold meant much to her, and she would not chance the displeasure of the Baron; she'd betray anyone who tried to rely on her against her liege.

Abilities
She was a competent bowyer and fletcher.

Activities
Clarrisa made bows, and arrows, and quarrels.

Relationships
The bowyer had a close friend named Darrold.

History
After the vampire Romanisk Vlaensk took control of Stag Falls one spring in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, he had her brought to the castle by Baron Vilhelm von Stag Falls, and forced to make arrows and quarrels day and night, by threatening her friend Darrold. Unknown to her, Darrold had already been killed. None of the other inhabitants of the castle knew of this threat held over her.

Adventurers sent from Ravens Bluff to investigate Stag Falls may have come to her while infiltrating the castle; if they did, she'd have warned the Baron unless they had proof Darrold was indeed dead.

## Clan Rookoath

Clan Rookoath was a clan of dwarves who resided in the Fairheight Range of Alaron within the Moonshae Isles.

History
Following the return of Sarifal in the late 14 century DR, Clan Rookoath offered shelter to the dwarves of Clan Rustfire when their home in Gwynneth was overrun by unseelie fey. Together they faced the threat of Rustfire's enemies as they attempted to attack Alaron via the Tunnels of Dwarvenhome.

By the late 15 century DR, the two clans were locked in a desperate war against the orcs, trolls, and other monsters of the mountains being led by a young shadow dragon. In the, they sent three scouts southward to petition King Derid Kendrick of Callidyrr for aid, who was not known to be aware of their plight. It was hoped that they could offer to aid him with his own struggles if he could first help save their realm. By the, adventurers had helped the dwarves to secure victories in their war, but the dwarves had come to reject any overtures of alliance from King Derid.

## Clan Rustfire

Clan Rustfire was a clan of dwarves who resided in the Moonshae Isles. As of the late 15 century DR, they shared the Fairheight Range of Alaron with Clan Rookoath.

History
Clan Rustfire had resided on Gwynneth as of the, however following the return of Sarifal, they were attacked by evil fey and driven from their home. They fled through the Tunnels of Dwarvenhome to join Clan Rookoath on Alaron, where their pursuers attempted to follow them.

By the late 15 century DR, the two clans were locked in a desperate war against the orcs, trolls, and other monsters of the mountains being led by a young shadow dragon. In the, they sent three scouts southward to petition King Derid Kendrick of Callidyrr for aid, who was not known to be aware of their plight. It was hoped that they could offer to aid him with his own struggles if he could first help save their realm. By the, adventurers had helped the dwarves to secure victories in their war, but the dwarves had come to reject any overtures of alliance from King Derid.

## Clash at Earthrift

The Clash at Earthrift was a battle of the Giant Wars that took place at what would be known as the Gorge of the Fallen Idol, fought between the giants of Nedeheim and the allied dwarves of Shanatar and elven kingdoms.

History
In order to mutually guard against their giantish foes, Shanatar allied with the nearby elven realms of Tethir and Darthiir, which also served to guard against the expansionist humans of Coramshan. Around  to  a great battle took place at Earthrift in High Shanatar, which brought the alliance together. A dwarven stratagem to crush the giants with a landslide succeeded in killing twenty of them, but it also slew a number of elves—among which were the two elven princes Tethir, the sons of the elven general. This disaster prompted the elves to leave the battle mid-way and so the giants won a bloody victory over the dwarves.

The alliance with Tethir was broken immediately, and the elves began a vendetta against the Gemcrypt clan of dwarves, eventually eliminating them from the western Starspire Mountains. The other elven realms also broke their alliances over the next century, leaving Shanatar to fight the giants and humans on their own.

Appendix
References

---

## Clan Gemcrypt

Clan Gemcrypt was an ancient dwarven clan of Shanatar in the sub-kingdom of Sondarr.

History
The clan emerged in the realm of Sondorr, and dominated the eastern end of the realm beneath the interior Starspire Mountains while Clan Ghalmrin and Clan Trollspear controlled regions to the west. The three clans' ardukes were the regents for the ruling Triumvirate for most of the realm's history following the death of King Ebersar. Gemcrypt lasted until not long after the disastrous Clash at Earthrift, when the accidental killing of two elven princes in the battle caused a deadly feud with the elves of the Forest of Tethir. This led to the extinction of the clan around the 50 century before Dale Reckoning.Powers & Pantheons claims that Gemcrypt controlled the port of Velmhold, but Drizzt Do'Urden's Guide to the Underdark claims it was Clan Trollspear's. As Trollspear were seafarers and Gemcrypt were far inland, this would be a simple error.

## Clenched Fist (mercenary company)

The Clenched Fist was a mercenary and adventuring company that was a subsidiary of the Rundeen mercantile organization in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. Active in the Shining Sea region, they served the Rundeen as enforcers and dungeon delvers.

Organization
The Clenched Fist, both the leaders, individual members, and the band as a whole, were sponsored and actively supported by the Rundeen. They were controlled directly by the First Yrshelem, the second tier of Rundeen leadership, but technically ranked below the Mitalibbar, their collectors and watchers, like their other subsidiaries. Although technically an independent company on a full-time retainer, the Clenched Fist were treated as employees and thus were effectively a semiprivate army.

The senior members of the Clenched Fist led the company and each commanded their own smaller bands or cells of 8–12 combatants. They selected their personnel according to capabilities, temperament, and suitability for a mission. The leaders understood they were simply muscle for the Rundeen but were happy with this arrangement, provided they and their underlings got their money.

The lower ranks were fighters and rogues, all lesser warriors and one-time gladiatorial slaves who'd all been bought by the Rundeen for the purpose. The company had well over 100 members as of 1370 DR.

Activities
The Rundeen leaders assigned the Clenched Fist a range of specific missions to carry out. These included such shady jobs pressuring creditors and members who'd fallen behind or fallen short in their payments; enforcing rules in Rundeen-controlled markets, both to maintain the influence of the Rundeen and limit that of others; banditry of caravans on trade routes; forcing "protection" on caravans; robbing the safe holds of rival merchants; and even exploring dungeons. For the latter, they were often supplied with maps, information, weapons, and armor, and would split the loot 50:50 with the Rundeen in exchange.

Tactics
They were renowned for their love of battle.

Relationships
The Clenched Fist were one of a number of mercenary and adventuring bands, along with the Fist of Rundeen and Many Long Arms, formed by the Rundeen with the express purpose of opposing the Harpers.

History
The Clenched Fist began as a dozen-strong band of thieves and warriors based in Schamedar, Calimshan. Rowdy and greedy, they took any job they came across just for the coin. That was until the Rundeen hired them on a permanent basis and started giving them missions as if they were salaried employees. The founding members didn't mind, as it meant a constant income.

They developed in experience and acquired more personnel, growing swiftly in only a few years. The original members, those who'd survived, worked as commanders of smaller bands of their own.

In the, the Clenched Fist fought a band of Harpers including the bard Mintiper Moonsilver, who had nothing good to say about them.In Empires of the Shining Sea pp. 171–172, Mintiper's quote is made in 1356 DR, so it is presumed the battle took place the same year. They would go on to have several clashes, but were defeated several times by the Harpers, who saw them as no longer effective by 1367 DR.

Despite this, the Clenched Fist remained in operation as of 1370 DR. Nine members of the original twelve still lived and led the company.

Members
Known leaders and founding members of the Clenched Fist were:
 Yusuf al Memnon, a human fighter and hairy bear of a man;
 El Yestaadi, "the Huntress", a half-elf fighter/thief, graceful and deadly;
 Daud, a human thief, an amnesiac and spy;
 Astil adh Saida al Manshaka, a human fighter, a former slave of great strength and tactical skill;
 Wagym, a wereboar fighter and former slave gladiator; and
 Crymara al Rundeen, a human thief, a seductress and knife thrower.
